Just me reacting to the facts of the situation. So, yeah, I honestly don't know if I'm clinically depressed or having significant anxiety or whatever the medical terms for it would be. I just know I'm completely miserable because of how crooked my jaw is, and it feels like it's never going to get better. I just feel totally stuck. Because like I was just saying a minute ago, they do tell me they do not see it. I just cannot stop thinking about it. I cannot turn off this absolute certainty that the left side of my face is completely crooked and messed up. It is not like I want to feel this way. I don't want to walk into the breakroom at work to grab a water and feel this intense, burning sensation that every single person eating their sandwich is staring right at my jawline. I don't want to be out on the dance floor, or well, back when I used to actually go to salsa night, trying to remember the steps, but all I can focus on is whether my partner is secretly grossed out by how lopsided my cheek is. \n\nEven when they swear up and down they don't see anything, my brain just screams at me that they are just being polite or looking the other way out of pity. I just can't stop thinking that everyone is noticing it. It is just always there, you know? Like this giant spotlight on the side of my face that only I can feel, but I am convinced everyone else is staring right at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the left side of your face is actually perfectly normal and your perception of it being crooked is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I feel like... My heart will start beating a little harder and my palms get a little sweaty. \n\nBut to me, that just feels like a normal reaction to knowing you look terrible and that people are going to be staring at you, you know? Like, if your face was this lopsided, you'd probably be stressed out about going out in public, too. So I don't know if that’s an actual anxiety condition or if it's just... reality. Just me reacting to the facts of the situation. So, yeah, I honestly don't know if I'm clinically depressed or having significant anxiety or whatever the medical terms for it would be. I just know I'm completely miserable because of how crooked my jaw is, and it feels like it's never going to get better. I just feel totally stuck. But I don't really have a clinical name for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your face being crooked might not match how others see you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know we just kind of touched on this, and I get what you are asking. You are asking if maybe I am seeing one thing and the rest of the world is seeing something completely different. And sure, logically, maybe? Because like I was just saying a minute ago, they do tell me they do not see it. The guys at the warehouse, the people at salsa night... they say my face looks fine. \n\nBut... and this is exactly why I am here, sitting in this chair talking to you right now... I just cannot stop thinking about it. I cannot turn off this absolute certainty that the left side of my face is completely crooked and messed up. It is not like I want to feel this way. I don't want to walk into the breakroom at work to grab a water and feel this intense, burning sensation that every single person eating their sandwich is staring right at my jawline. I don't want to be out on the dance floor, or well, back when I used to actually go to salsa night, trying to remember the steps, but all I can focus on is whether my partner is secretly grossed out by how lopsided my cheek is. \n\nEven when they swear up and down they don't see anything, my brain just screams at me that they are just being polite or looking the other way out of pity. So, is it possible my perception doesn't match theirs? I guess, technically, sure. But it doesn't change anything for me in my day-to-day life. It doesn't stop the thoughts from running a mile a minute. I am just trapped in my own head, constantly obsessing over how messed up it looks, and no matter how many times someone says I look normal, it just bounces right off me. I just can't stop thinking that everyone is noticing it. It is just always there, you know? Like this giant spotlight on the side of my face that only I can feel, but I am convinced everyone else is staring right at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the left side of your face is actually perfectly normal and your perception of it being crooked is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I feel like... I mean, I know we're kind of going over the exact same ground here, doc. Like I was just saying, I get the logic of what you're asking. You're asking if I'm just straight-up making this up in my head or something, if it's not based in reality at all. But... to say it's \"perfectly normal\"? I just, I can't wrap my head around that. I really can't. \n\nBecause, look, the whole reason I made this appointment, the whole reason I'm even sitting in this office right now, is because I just can't stop thinking that the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. I can't just turn the volume down on it. It’s not like I wake up and think, \"Oh, maybe it's fine today.\" It is constantly—and I mean constantly—on my mind. \n\nI'll be walking down the main aisle at the warehouse, carrying a heavy box of inventory or pushing a cart, and I'm just bracing myself because I feel like everyone I pass is noticing it. Or, you know, like I mentioned with salsa night... I'd be in the middle of a crowded floor, the music is blasting, people are moving everywhere, and instead of just enjoying the dance, I'm completely consumed by this feeling that my partner is staring right at my lopsided jaw. And like I said before, even when they explicitly tell me they don't see anything, even when they promise me I look fine... it doesn't matter. The thoughts don't stop. I just keep thinking they're noticing it and just lying to my face to spare my feelings. \n\nSo... is it possible my face is perfectly normal and this isn't real? I don't even know how to answer that anymore. All I know is I can't stop obsessing over it. It's like I'm trapped in this loop where my face is messed up, everyone sees it, and nothing anyone says can convince me otherwise. It just... it ruins my whole day, every single day. From the minute I wake up to the minute I clock out at the warehouse, it's all I can focus on.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think it's possible that your face appears normal to others, but you're seeing it differently?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 30 / stocker_or_order_filler / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Preoccupation_with_Appearance\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because I can’t stop thinking that the left side of my face looks crooked and messed up, like everyone at the warehouse or at salsa night is noticing it even when they tell me they don’t see anything.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doc, I... I mean, respectfully, we are literally going in circles here, man. You keep asking me the same question just wrapped up in different words, about whether I'm seeing things differently than everyone else. And I keep trying to explain to you that I’m here, sitting in this chair, specifically because I can’t stop thinking that the left side of my face looks totally crooked and messed up. That is the entire reason I came to see a doctor in the first place. 

It’s like a broken record playing in my head that I can't unplug. Whether I'm at the warehouse scanning barcodes and wrapping pallets, or if I'm trying to go to salsa night just to have one normal evening... I just have this overwhelming, sickening feeling that everyone is noticing it. Every single person. And yeah, I know I already told you that they tell me they don’t see anything. The guys at work will be like, "Man, you look fine, just pass the tape gun," or my dance partners will swear they didn't even notice my jaw. But it literally does not matter what they say to me. I hear the words coming out of their mouths, but my brain just completely rejects it. 

Because when I look in the mirror before I leave the apartment, it's right there. It's so obvious, so glaringly messed up. So when you ask if it's possible my face is normal and I'm just seeing it differently... I mean, maybe? But how am I supposed to just accept that when the feeling is this intense? It's not just a passing thought, it's like this heavy, suffocating blanket over my entire life. I can't even focus on stacking boxes right because I'm too busy angling my head a certain way so my coworkers don't catch the bad side. I can't enjoy the rhythm of the music at the club because I'm sweating bullets, completely convinced everyone is staring at my lopsided cheek. So, I honestly don't know what else to tell you. I'm just here because I can't stop obsessing over it, and it feels like it's ruining my life, no matter how many times people swear to me that I look normal.

The way the left cheekbone seems lower, or maybe it's the jawline on that side that's just pulled down, making the whole thing look totally lopsided. I can spend, like, hours just staring at that one side, trying to figure out if it's getting worse, or if it's always been this bad and I just didn't notice it as much before. I'll take pictures on my phone from different angles, trying to catch it in different lighting, just to prove to myself that I'm not crazy, that it really is as crooked as I think it is. But yeah, if you're asking if I have issues with anything below the neck, or even the right side of my face, no. It's just this one terrible, glaring flaw on the left side that I can't escape. It's like my eyes are just drawn to it immediately, and I can't look at anything else when I see my reflection.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the left side of your face caused you to avoid any social or work-related activities?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah... yeah, it has. It really has, and it's honestly ruining everything. I mean, at work, it's gotten so bad that I've actually been written up. My supervisor pulled me into the office last week because my numbers were down. And I tried to explain that I was just... distracted, but I couldn't tell him the real reason. The truth is, I keep stopping to check my reflection in the breakroom mirror, or I'll go to the bathroom just to look at my face under the lights in there, trying to see if the shadows make it look worse. And then I get stuck there, you know? I'll be in there for twenty minutes, just staring, trying to push my skin up to see if it fixes the crookedness, and meanwhile, my orders are piling up. So yeah, my work is definitely suffering because I just can't focus on pulling stock when I know everyone walking past me is probably staring at my face.\n\nAnd outside of work, it's... it's basically stopped my life completely. I told the guys I pulled a hamstring, but really, I just couldn't stand being out on the field in the bright daylight where there's nowhere to hide my face. \n\nIt's not just hobbies, either. I've been skipping family gatherings, too. My sister had a barbecue a couple of weeks ago for her birthday, and I made up some excuse about feeling sick. But the real reason was that I knew my aunt would be there with her iPad, taking a million pictures like she always does. And the thought of my face, that crooked left side, being captured in a photo that's going to end up on Facebook for everyone to scrutinize... I just couldn't go. I stayed home and basically just sat in the dark. So, yeah, I'm avoiding pretty much everything now because I just can't stand the thought of people looking at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions or rituals in an attempt to alleviate your concerns about the left side of your face?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I definitely do. It's like I can't control it, honestly. I know I mentioned the breakroom earlier, but it’s really bad in the actual restrooms at the warehouse. Like, right before I have to go back out on the inventory floor—after my fifteen-minute break or my lunch, or even if I just sneak away to use the urinal—I get completely trapped at the sinks. I just keep checking my face in that bathroom mirror. The lighting in there is this sickly, harsh fluorescent, and I just stand there, leaning over the sink, staring right at the left side of my face. \n\nAnd I wear this company-issued baseball cap, right? We all have to wear them. So I’ll stand there fixing my cap, pulling the brim down low over my eyes, then pushing it back up, tilting it to the left, trying to see if I can angle the shadow to hide how crooked my cheek is. I’ll adjust it a dozen times. And while I'm doing that, I just start rubbing at my face. It’s always this one exact spot on the left side, right along the jawline. I just keep rubbing and pressing on it with my thumb, almost like... I don't know, like I’m hoping if I press hard enough, I can push the bone back into place or smooth out the lopsidedness. I’ll rub it until the skin gets all raw and red, which I know probably draws more attention to it, but in the moment, I just can't stop myself from doing it. \n\nAnd the worst part is, I lose all track of time. I'll think I've been in there for two minutes, but it's been twenty. So I end up being super late getting back to my aisle. At the warehouse, everything is tracked by these handheld scanners, so they know exactly how many items you pull per hour. Because I'm stuck in the bathroom doing this whole routine with the mirror and the hat and rubbing my face, my picking numbers just completely fall behind. And then my boss gets on me again. He’ll come track me down in the aisles with his clipboard, asking why my pick rate is in the toilet for the last hour and where I wandered off to. I can't exactly tell him the truth, so I just have to stand there and take it, knowing I'm probably going to do the exact same thing the next time I walk past a mirror.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other areas of your life, beyond work and social activities, where you feel your concerns about your face are affecting your ability to function or causing you distress?\n\nPatient: I mean, when you say other areas... I'm trying to think, but honestly, what else is there? Like, work and social stuff, that basically makes up a person's entire life, you know? At least, it makes up mine. I’m a single guy, thirty years old, I don't have kids or a mortgage or anything like that. So my routine has always just been waking up, going to the warehouse, putting in my hours, and then having a little fun on the weekends with the soccer guys or Thursday nights with the salsa dancing. It's just... a void, I guess. \n\nI mean, I suppose you could say it affects my downtime at home, if that counts as an area of life? They tell me they don't see anything. They'll say, \"Man, you look fine, what are you even talking about?\" \n\nAnd for like, a split second, I want to believe them. So even when they look me dead in the eye and swear they don't see anything wrong, my brain just tells me they're lying. I figure they're just being polite, or they pity me, or they just don't want to make things awkward by agreeing that I look terrible. \n\nBecause I just can't stop thinking about it. It's like this tape playing on a loop in my head every single second of the day. I’m just constantly convinced that everyone at the warehouse is noticing it when I walk down the aisle, or that everyone at salsa night was staring right at my lopsided cheek the whole time we were dancing. So, no matter what anyone tells me, I just... I can't shake it. That's why I'm here, honestly, because I just can't stop obsessing over how crooked and messed up my face is, and I can't turn off the feeling that everyone is staring at it, even when they swear to me they don't see a thing.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing significant anxiety or depression as a result of your concerns about your face?\n\nPatient: Anxiety or depression... I mean, I don't really know how to answer that. I'm not entirely sure what counts as, like, official depression or a real anxiety problem, you know? I've never been the kind of guy to go to a therapist or read up on mental health stuff before all this. I just... I know I feel like absolute garbage most of the time. But is it depression? I really couldn't tell you. I still get out of bed, I still put on my steel-toe boots and my safety vest and drive my car to the warehouse every single morning. I'm not just laying under the covers crying all day or anything like that. \n\nBut at the same time, it’s just this heavy, exhausting feeling that never really goes away. Like, I was saying earlier about sitting alone in my apartment... I'll just sit on this old brown hand-me-down couch my sister gave me when I moved in, and I just feel completely drained. Just wiped out. Like there's this physical weight sitting right on my chest. My heart will start beating a little harder and my palms get a little sweaty. \n\nBut to me, that just feels like a normal reaction to knowing you look terrible and that people are going to be staring at you, you know? Just me reacting to the facts of the situation. So, yeah, I honestly don't know if I'm clinically depressed or having significant anxiety or whatever the medical terms for it would be. I just know I'm completely miserable because of how crooked my jaw is, and it feels like it's never going to get better. I just feel totally stuck. But I don't really have a clinical name for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your face being crooked might not match how others see you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know we just kind of touched on this, and I get what you are asking. You are asking if maybe I am seeing one thing and the rest of the world is seeing something completely different. And sure, logically, maybe? Because like I was just saying a minute ago, they do tell me they do not see it. The guys at the warehouse, the people at salsa night... they say my face looks fine. \n\nBut... and this is exactly why I am here, sitting in this chair talking to you right now... I just cannot stop thinking about it. I cannot turn off this absolute certainty that the left side of my face is completely crooked and messed up. It is not like I want to feel this way. I don't want to walk into the breakroom at work to grab a water and feel this intense, burning sensation that every single person eating their sandwich is staring right at my jawline. I don't want to be out on the dance floor, or well, back when I used to actually go to salsa night, trying to remember the steps, but all I can focus on is whether my partner is secretly grossed out by how lopsided my cheek is. \n\nEven when they swear up and down they don't see anything, my brain just screams at me that they are just being polite or looking the other way out of pity. So, is it possible my perception doesn't match theirs? I guess, technically, sure. But it doesn't change anything for me in my day-to-day life. It doesn't stop the thoughts from running a mile a minute. I am just trapped in my own head, constantly obsessing over how messed up it looks, and no matter how many times someone says I look normal, it just bounces right off me. I just can't stop thinking that everyone is noticing it. It is just always there, you know? Like this giant spotlight on the side of my face that only I can feel, but I am convinced everyone else is staring right at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the left side of your face is actually perfectly normal and your perception of it being crooked is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I feel like... I mean, I know we're kind of going over the exact same ground here, doc. Like I was just saying, I get the logic of what you're asking. You're asking if I'm just straight-up making this up in my head or something, if it's not based in reality at all. But... to say it's \"perfectly normal\"? I just, I can't wrap my head around that. I really can't. \n\nBecause, look, the whole reason I made this appointment, the whole reason I'm even sitting in this office right now, is because I just can't stop thinking that the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. I can't just turn the volume down on it. It’s not like I wake up and think, \"Oh, maybe it's fine today.\" It is constantly—and I mean constantly—on my mind. \n\nI'll be walking down the main aisle at the warehouse, carrying a heavy box of inventory or pushing a cart, and I'm just bracing myself because I feel like everyone I pass is noticing it. Or, you know, like I mentioned with salsa night... I just keep thinking they're noticing it and just lying to my face to spare my feelings. \n\nSo... is it possible my face is perfectly normal and this isn't real? I don't even know how to answer that anymore. All I know is I can't stop obsessing over it. It's like I'm trapped in this loop where my face is messed up, everyone sees it, and nothing anyone says can convince me otherwise. It just... it ruins my whole day, every single day. From the minute I wake up to the minute I clock out at the warehouse, it's all I can focus on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your face appears normal to others, but you're seeing it differently?\n\nPatient: Doc, I... I mean, respectfully, we are literally going in circles here, man. You keep asking me the same question just wrapped up in different words, about whether I'm seeing things differently than everyone else. And I keep trying to explain to you that I’m here, sitting in this chair, specifically because I can’t stop thinking that the left side of my face looks totally crooked and messed up. That is the entire reason I came to see a doctor in the first place. \n\nIt’s like a broken record playing in my head that I can't unplug. Whether I'm at the warehouse scanning barcodes and wrapping pallets, or if I'm trying to go to salsa night just to have one normal evening... I just have this overwhelming, sickening feeling that everyone is noticing it. Every single person. And yeah, I know I already told you that they tell me they don’t see anything. The guys at work will be like, \"Man, you look fine, just pass the tape gun,\" or my dance partners will swear they didn't even notice my jaw. But it literally does not matter what they say to me. I hear the words coming out of their mouths, but my brain just completely rejects it. \n\nBecause when I look in the mirror before I leave the apartment, it's right there. It's so obvious, so glaringly messed up. So when you ask if it's possible my face is normal and I'm just seeing it differently... I mean, maybe? But how am I supposed to just accept that when the feeling is this intense? It's not just a passing thought, it's like this heavy, suffocating blanket over my entire life. I can't even focus on stacking boxes right because I'm too busy angling my head a certain way so my coworkers don't catch the bad side. I can't enjoy the rhythm of the music at the club because I'm sweating bullets, completely convinced everyone is staring at my lopsided cheek. So, I honestly don't know what else to tell you. I don't want to be out on the dance floor, or well, back when I used to actually go to salsa night, trying to remember the steps, but all I can focus on is whether my partner is secretly grossed out by how lopsided my cheek is. \n\nEven when they swear up and down they don't see anything, my brain just screams at me that they are just being polite or looking the other way out of pity. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doc, are you... are you messing with me right now? Seriously, man, I feel like I'm taking crazy pills here. You're asking me the exact same thing for the fourth time. Is this a test? Because I honestly don't know how many different ways I can answer this for you.

That is the bottom line. Whether my perception is technically off or not, it doesn't change the fact that this is destroying my day-to-day life. I can't escape it. I'll be at the warehouse, literally just trying to load a truck or wrap a pallet so I can pay my rent, or I'll be trying to force myself to go to salsa night just to feel like a normal guy for two seconds... and the whole time, my stomach is in absolute knots because I feel like everyone is noticing it. Everyone. Every single person who glances in my direction. 

And yes, like we've established over and over, even when they look me dead in the eye and tell me they don't see anything wrong with my face, it doesn't fix it. It doesn't make the thoughts go away or make me believe them. It's just this constant, heavy obsession that I can't shake, no matter how you phrase the question or how many times we go over it. I just can't stop thinking about it, man. It's always there.

I stayed home and basically just sat in the dark. So, yeah, I'm avoiding pretty much everything now because I just can't stand the thought of people looking at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions or rituals in an attempt to alleviate your concerns about the left side of your face?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I definitely do. It's like I can't control it, honestly. I know I mentioned the breakroom earlier, but it’s really bad in the actual restrooms at the warehouse. Like, right before I have to go back out on the inventory floor—after my fifteen-minute break or my lunch, or even if I just sneak away to use the urinal—I get completely trapped at the sinks. I just keep checking my face in that bathroom mirror. I just keep rubbing and pressing on it with my thumb, almost like... I don't know, like I’m hoping if I press hard enough, I can push the bone back into place or smooth out the lopsidedness. I’ll rub it until the skin gets all raw and red, which I know probably draws more attention to it, but in the moment, I just can't stop myself from doing it. \n\nAnd the worst part is, I lose all track of time. I'll think I've been in there for two minutes, but it's been twenty. So I end up being super late getting back to my aisle. At the warehouse, everything is tracked by these handheld scanners, so they know exactly how many items you pull per hour. He’ll come track me down in the aisles with his clipboard, asking why my pick rate is in the toilet for the last hour and where I wandered off to. I can't exactly tell him the truth, so I just have to stand there and take it, knowing I'm probably going to do the exact same thing the next time I walk past a mirror.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other areas of your life, beyond work and social activities, where you feel your concerns about your face are affecting your ability to function or causing you distress?\n\nPatient: I mean, when you say other areas... I'm trying to think, but honestly, what else is there? Like, work and social stuff, that basically makes up a person's entire life, you know? At least, it makes up mine. I’m a single guy, thirty years old, I don't have kids or a mortgage or anything like that. Just wiped out. Like there's this physical weight sitting right on my chest. Just me reacting to the facts of the situation. So, yeah, I honestly don't know if I'm clinically depressed or having significant anxiety or whatever the medical terms for it would be. I just know I'm completely miserable because of how crooked my jaw is, and it feels like it's never going to get better. I just feel totally stuck. I just can't stop thinking that everyone is noticing it. It is just always there, you know? I mean, I know we're kind of going over the exact same ground here, doc. Like I was just saying, I get the logic of what you're asking. You're asking if I'm just straight-up making this up in my head or something, if it's not based in reality at all. But... to say it's \"perfectly normal\"? I just, I can't wrap my head around that. I really can't. \n\nBecause, look, the whole reason I made this appointment, the whole reason I'm even sitting in this office right now, is because I just can't stop thinking that the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. I can't just turn the volume down on it. It’s not like I wake up and think, \"Oh, maybe it's fine today.\" It is constantly—and I mean constantly—on my mind. \n\nI'll be walking down the main aisle at the warehouse, carrying a heavy box of inventory or pushing a cart, and I'm just bracing myself because I feel like everyone I pass is noticing it. I just keep thinking they're noticing it and just lying to my face to spare my feelings. \n\nSo... is it possible my face is perfectly normal and this isn't real? I don't even know how to answer that anymore. All I know is I can't stop obsessing over it. It's like I'm trapped in this loop where my face is messed up, everyone sees it, and nothing anyone says can convince me otherwise. It just... it ruins my whole day, every single day. From the minute I wake up to the minute I clock out at the warehouse, it's all I can focus on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your face appears normal to others, but you're seeing it differently?\n\nPatient: Doc, I... I mean, respectfully, we are literally going in circles here, man. You keep asking me the same question just wrapped up in different words, about whether I'm seeing things differently than everyone else. And I keep trying to explain to you that I’m here, sitting in this chair, specifically because I can’t stop thinking that the left side of my face looks totally crooked and messed up. That is the entire reason I came to see a doctor in the first place. \n\nIt’s like a broken record playing in my head that I can't unplug. Whether I'm at the warehouse scanning barcodes and wrapping pallets, or if I'm trying to go to salsa night just to have one normal evening... I just have this overwhelming, sickening feeling that everyone is noticing it. Every single person. And yeah, I know I already told you that they tell me they don’t see anything. The guys at work will be like, \"Man, you look fine, just pass the tape gun,\" or my dance partners will swear they didn't even notice my jaw. But it literally does not matter what they say to me. I hear the words coming out of their mouths, but my brain just completely rejects it. \n\nBecause when I look in the mirror before I leave the apartment, it's right there. It's so obvious, so glaringly messed up. So when you ask if it's possible my face is normal and I'm just seeing it differently... I mean, maybe? But how am I supposed to just accept that when the feeling is this intense? It's not just a passing thought, it's like this heavy, suffocating blanket over my entire life. I can't even focus on stacking boxes right because I'm too busy angling my head a certain way so my coworkers don't catch the bad side. I can't enjoy the rhythm of the music at the club because I'm sweating bullets, completely convinced everyone is staring at my lopsided cheek. So, I honestly don't know what else to tell you. I'm just here because I can't stop obsessing over it, and it feels like it's ruining my life, no matter how many times people swear to me that I look normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your face is not based on how it actually looks?\n\nPatient: Doc, are you... are you messing with me right now? Seriously, man, I feel like I'm taking crazy pills here. You're asking me the exact same thing for the fourth time. Is this a test? Because I honestly don't know how many different ways I can answer this for you. \n\nLook, like I have been saying this whole entire time... Everyone. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, man... "harder to enjoy" doesn't even begin to cover it. It's... it's honestly bleeding into every single corner of my life right now. 

Like I was telling you about salsa night earlier, right? Well, the truth is, I haven't actually been in weeks. I used to go every Thursday, religiously. I loved the music, the energy, just... getting out of my own head for a few hours. But now, just the thought of being under those club lights, knowing everyone is going to be staring at my face... I just can't do it. I've started skipping it entirely. 

And it's not just dancing. I play—well, I *used* to play—in this Sunday league soccer thing with some guys from the neighborhood. But I stopped showing up to that, too. And family gatherings? Forget about it. My sister had a get-together recently and I just made up some excuse about feeling sick, because I know my aunt is always pulling out her phone to take pictures. The idea of my crooked jaw being immortalized in a photo, where everyone can just zoom in and stare at how messed up it is... it literally makes my stomach turn. I just can't deal with people looking at me or taking pictures anymore. It's way too much pressure.

And it’s not even just the fun stuff, doc. It’s my job. It's my actual livelihood. I told you how I'm always trying to hide my face at work, angling my head when I'm pushing the cart or wrapping a pallet? Well, it's actually getting me in real trouble now. Because I'm so distracted, so intensely focused on making sure nobody is catching my bad side, I'm moving way slower. I'll literally stand there at the end of an aisle, waiting for people to pass before I go grab an item, just so they don't see me. And because of that, my work gets delayed. My supervisor actually pulled me into the office the other day and wrote me up. He formally wrote me up because my numbers are down. I've been at that warehouse for years and I've never had a write-up! But I just can't focus on filling orders when my brain is screaming at me all day. 

So... yeah. I'm not enjoying anything. I'm just hiding.

That is the entire reason I came to see a doctor in the first place. \n\nIt’s like a broken record playing in my head that I can't unplug. Whether I'm at the warehouse scanning barcodes and wrapping pallets, or if I'm trying to go to salsa night just to have one normal evening... I just have this overwhelming, sickening feeling that everyone is noticing it. Every single person. And yeah, I know I already told you that they tell me they don’t see anything. The guys at work will be like, \"Man, you look fine, just pass the tape gun,\" or my dance partners will swear they didn't even notice my jaw. But it literally does not matter what they say to me. I hear the words coming out of their mouths, but my brain just completely rejects it. \n\nBecause when I look in the mirror before I leave the apartment, it's right there. It's so obvious, so glaringly messed up. So when you ask if it's possible my face is normal and I'm just seeing it differently... I mean, maybe? But how am I supposed to just accept that when the feeling is this intense? It's not just a passing thought, it's like this heavy, suffocating blanket over my entire life. I can't even focus on stacking boxes right because I'm too busy angling my head a certain way so my coworkers don't catch the bad side. I can't enjoy the rhythm of the music at the club because I'm sweating bullets, completely convinced everyone is staring at my lopsided cheek. So, I honestly don't know what else to tell you. I'm just here because I can't stop obsessing over it, and it feels like it's ruining my life, no matter how many times people swear to me that I look normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your face is not based on how it actually looks?\n\nPatient: Doc, are you... are you messing with me right now? Because I honestly don't know how many different ways I can answer this for you. \n\nLook, like I have been saying this whole entire time... I am here, in this office, because I just cannot stop thinking that the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. That is the bottom line. Whether my perception is technically off or not, it doesn't change the fact that this is destroying my day-to-day life. I can't escape it. I'll be at the warehouse, literally just trying to load a truck or wrap a pallet so I can pay my rent, or I'll be trying to force myself to go to salsa night just to feel like a normal guy for two seconds... and the whole time, my stomach is in absolute knots because I feel like everyone is noticing it. Everyone. I just can't stop thinking about it, man. It's always there.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to enjoy things you used to like because of your concerns about your face?\n\nPatient: Oh, man... \"harder to enjoy\" doesn't even begin to cover it. But now, just the thought of being under those club lights, knowing everyone is going to be staring at my face... I just can't do it. I've started skipping it entirely. \n\nAnd it's not just dancing. I play—well, I *used* to play—in this Sunday league soccer thing with some guys from the neighborhood. But I stopped showing up to that, too. And family gatherings? Forget about it. My sister had a get-together recently and I just made up some excuse about feeling sick, because I know my aunt is always pulling out her phone to take pictures. The idea of my crooked jaw being immortalized in a photo, where everyone can just zoom in and stare at how messed up it is... it literally makes my stomach turn. I just can't deal with people looking at me or taking pictures anymore. It's way too much pressure.\n\nAnd it’s not even just the fun stuff, doc. It’s my job. It's my actual livelihood. I told you how I'm always trying to hide my face at work, angling my head when I'm pushing the cart or wrapping a pallet? Well, it's actually getting me in real trouble now. Because I'm so distracted, so intensely focused on making sure nobody is catching my bad side, I'm moving way slower. I'll literally stand there at the end of an aisle, waiting for people to pass before I go grab an item, just so they don't see me. And because of that, my work gets delayed. My supervisor actually pulled me into the office the other day and wrote me up. He formally wrote me up because my numbers are down. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, doc... I mean, yes. That is exactly what I literally just laid out for you. I don't know if I'm just not explaining it right, or if you have a checklist you have to get through, but yes, it is significantly interfering.

And then my supervisor is on my case, pulling me into the office, and I have a literal disciplinary paper in my file now after years of being a good worker. Over this. Because I can't focus.

And my personal life? It's the exact same thing. I’ve started skipping everything—the Sunday soccer games with the guys, the Thursday salsa nights, even my own family gatherings. I just... I can't handle it. I can't put myself in a situation where I have to deal with people looking at me, or my aunt pulling out her phone and taking pictures of everyone. The thought of a camera flashing and catching my face, freezing how messed up it is so everyone can just stare at it later... it makes me want to crawl out of my skin. I can't do it. 

So yeah, asking if it's "interfering with my ability to function" is kind of an understatement. It's basically stopped my entire life in its tracks. I don't function like a normal person anymore, because every single decision I make is based on how I can avoid people seeing my face.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, doctor, I’m just... I’m at my wit's end here. I'm here because I just can't stop thinking about my face. Specifically, it's the left side of my face. It's just completely crooked and... and messed up. Like, if you look at it—well, maybe the lighting in here isn't showing it exactly right, but I know it's there. It's like the whole left side just doesn't line up with the right side at all. \n\nAnd it's ruining my life, really. I work as an order filler at this massive warehouse, right? And the fluorescent lights in there are brutal, they cast these harsh shadows, and I just know everyone on my shift is staring at it. I catch them looking when I'm walking down the aisles. And then, I try to have a life outside of work, you know, I go to this salsa night on Thursdays—or I used to go a lot more before this got so bad—and when you're dancing, you're up close with people, right in their space. I can literally feel them staring at my crooked face. \n\nThe part that makes me feel like I'm losing my mind, though, is that whenever I bring it up to the guys at the warehouse or my dance partners, they swear they don't see anything. They tell me I look fine, that they don't see what I'm talking about. But I know they're just being polite, you know? They have to be noticing it because it's so obvious. It's all I can see, and I just can't stop obsessing over it. It's constantly on my mind from the minute I wake up until I go to sleep, just wondering how bad it looks to everyone else...\n\nDoctor: Is it only your face, specifically the left side, that you're concerned about, or are there other parts of your body that you worry are flawed as well?\n\nPatient: No, it's... it's just my face. Just that left side. I mean, I don't really care about the rest of my body, you know? Like, my body is fine. I'm on my feet all day at the warehouse, lifting boxes, moving pallets, so I'm in decent shape. I don't really think about my arms or my stomach or anything like that. I don't look in the mirror and worry about my weight or if my shoulders are broad enough or whatever other guys worry about. It's not like that at all. \n\nIt's entirely focused on this one specific area. It's just... it's the asymmetry of it, right? The way the left cheekbone seems lower, or maybe it's the jawline on that side that's just pulled down, making the whole thing look totally lopsided. I can spend, like, hours just staring at that one side, trying to figure out if it's getting worse, or if it's always been this bad and I just didn't notice it as much before. I'll take pictures on my phone from different angles, trying to catch it in different lighting, just to prove to myself that I'm not crazy, that it really is as crooked as I think it is. But yeah, if you're asking if I have issues with anything below the neck, or even the right side of my face, no. It's just this one terrible, glaring flaw on the left side that I can't escape. It's like my eyes are just drawn to it immediately, and I can't look at anything else when I see my reflection.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the left side of your face caused you to avoid any social or work-related activities?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah... yeah, it has. It really has, and it's honestly ruining everything. I mean, at work, it's gotten so bad that I've actually been written up. My supervisor pulled me into the office last week because my numbers were down. And I tried to explain that I was just... distracted, but I couldn't tell him the real reason. The truth is, I keep stopping to check my reflection in the breakroom mirror, or I'll go to the bathroom just to look at my face under the lights in there, trying to see if the shadows make it look worse. And then I get stuck there, you know? I'll be in there for twenty minutes, just staring, trying to push my skin up to see if it fixes the crookedness, and meanwhile, my orders are piling up. So yeah, my work is definitely suffering because I just can't focus on pulling stock when I know everyone walking past me is probably staring at my face.\n\nAnd outside of work, it's... it's basically stopped my life completely. Like I mentioned before, I used to go to those salsa nights on Thursdays, right? I loved it. I was getting really good, actually. But I haven't been in over a month. I just can't do it anymore. The thought of being that close to a partner, with them looking right at me... I can't handle it. I even stopped playing in my Sunday soccer league. I told the guys I pulled a hamstring, but really, I just couldn't stand being out on the field in the bright daylight where there's nowhere to hide my face. \n\nIt's not just hobbies, either. I've been skipping family gatherings, too. My sister had a barbecue a couple of weeks ago for her birthday, and I made up some excuse about feeling sick. But the real reason was that I knew my aunt would be there with her iPad, taking a million pictures like she always does. And the thought of my face, that crooked left side, being captured in a photo that's going to end up on Facebook for everyone to scrutinize... I just couldn't go. I stayed home and basically just sat in the dark. So, yeah, I'm avoiding pretty much everything now because I just can't stand the thought of people looking at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions or rituals in an attempt to alleviate your concerns about the left side of your face?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I definitely do. It's like I can't control it, honestly. I know I mentioned the breakroom earlier, but it’s really bad in the actual restrooms at the warehouse. Like, right before I have to go back out on the inventory floor—after my fifteen-minute break or my lunch, or even if I just sneak away to use the urinal—I get completely trapped at the sinks. I just keep checking my face in that bathroom mirror. The lighting in there is this sickly, harsh fluorescent, and I just stand there, leaning over the sink, staring right at the left side of my face. \n\nAnd I wear this company-issued baseball cap, right? We all have to wear them. So I’ll stand there fixing my cap, pulling the brim down low over my eyes, then pushing it back up, tilting it to the left, trying to see if I can angle the shadow to hide how crooked my cheek is. I’ll adjust it a dozen times. And while I'm doing that, I just start rubbing at my face. It’s always this one exact spot on the left side, right along the jawline. I just keep rubbing and pressing on it with my thumb, almost like... I don't know, like I’m hoping if I press hard enough, I can push the bone back into place or smooth out the lopsidedness. I’ll rub it until the skin gets all raw and red, which I know probably draws more attention to it, but in the moment, I just can't stop myself from doing it. \n\nAnd the worst part is, I lose all track of time. I'll think I've been in there for two minutes, but it's been twenty. So I end up being super late getting back to my aisle. At the warehouse, everything is tracked by these handheld scanners, so they know exactly how many items you pull per hour. Because I'm stuck in the bathroom doing this whole routine with the mirror and the hat and rubbing my face, my picking numbers just completely fall behind. And then my boss gets on me again. He’ll come track me down in the aisles with his clipboard, asking why my pick rate is in the toilet for the last hour and where I wandered off to. I can't exactly tell him the truth, so I just have to stand there and take it, knowing I'm probably going to do the exact same thing the next time I walk past a mirror.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other areas of your life, beyond work and social activities, where you feel your concerns about your face are affecting your ability to function or causing you distress?\n\nPatient: I mean, when you say other areas... I'm trying to think, but honestly, what else is there? Like, work and social stuff, that basically makes up a person's entire life, you know? At least, it makes up mine. I’m a single guy, thirty years old, I don't have kids or a mortgage or anything like that. So my routine has always just been waking up, going to the warehouse, putting in my hours, and then having a little fun on the weekends with the soccer guys or Thursday nights with the salsa dancing. But now that both of those things are completely falling apart... I don't really have anything else. It's just... a void, I guess. \n\nI mean, I suppose you could say it affects my downtime at home, if that counts as an area of life? Because even when I'm completely alone in my apartment, with the blinds pulled down and no one around to look at me or judge me, I'm still not relaxed. I'm still just sitting on the edge of my bed or on the couch, pulling up the front-facing camera on my phone, staring at the screen, zooming in on my left cheekbone. I'll try to watch a movie or play a video game or something just to distract myself, but my mind just keeps drifting right back to my jawline. I'll literally pause the TV just to go into the bathroom and check the mirror again, even though I know I'm the only one in the apartment. So, I guess it ruins my peace of mind, my ability to just sit and exist without panicking about how lopsided my face is. \n\nBut as far as like, other actual responsibilities or functioning? No, not really. I still manage to pay my rent online, I still do my laundry, even if I'm just rushing down to the apartment complex basement late at night when I think no one else is down there so I don't have to make eye contact. I'm not failing at basic survival or anything. It's just that the things that actually made life feel like a life—hanging out with my sister, playing sports, doing a good job at work and maybe moving up to get my forklift certification someday—all of that is just stalled out. It feels like it's gone. Because I can't get past this one glaring thing on my face. So, yeah, I think work and being around people pretty much covers the extent of the damage, because without those, I'm really just a guy sitting alone in a room obsessing over his reflection.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe your face isn't actually as crooked as you think it is?\n\nPatient: I mean... I try to tell myself that sometimes. I really do try to rationalize it. Because, honestly, that's what other people say. If I ever kind of hint at it, or if I've flat out asked one of the guys at the warehouse, or back when I used to go to salsa night and I'd ask a partner if I looked alright... they always say the exact same thing. They tell me they don't see anything. They'll say, \"Man, you look fine, what are you even talking about?\" \n\nAnd for like, a split second, I want to believe them. I want to think, okay, maybe I'm blowing this out of proportion, maybe it's not that bad. But then... the feeling just comes rushing right back. Because I know what I see, you know? I can't just ignore my own eyes. I look in the mirror and the left side of my face is just so clearly crooked and messed up. It's glaring to me. So even when they look me dead in the eye and swear they don't see anything wrong, my brain just tells me they're lying. I figure they're just being polite, or they pity me, or they just don't want to make things awkward by agreeing that I look terrible. \n\nBecause I just can't stop thinking about it. It's like this tape playing on a loop in my head every single second of the day. I’m just constantly convinced that everyone at the warehouse is noticing it when I walk down the aisle, or that everyone at salsa night was staring right at my lopsided cheek the whole time we were dancing. So, no matter what anyone tells me, I just... I can't shake it. That's why I'm here, honestly, because I just can't stop obsessing over how crooked and messed up my face is, and I can't turn off the feeling that everyone is staring at it, even when they swear to me they don't see a thing.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing significant anxiety or depression as a result of your concerns about your face?\n\nPatient: Anxiety or depression... I mean, I don't really know how to answer that. I'm not entirely sure what counts as, like, official depression or a real anxiety problem, you know? I've never been the kind of guy to go to a therapist or read up on mental health stuff before all this. I just... I know I feel like absolute garbage most of the time. But is it depression? I really couldn't tell you. I still get out of bed, I still put on my steel-toe boots and my safety vest and drive my car to the warehouse every single morning. I'm not just laying under the covers crying all day or anything like that. \n\nBut at the same time, it’s just this heavy, exhausting feeling that never really goes away. Just wiped out. Like there's this physical weight sitting right on my chest. And when I think about having to go back to work the next day and punch in at the time clock, knowing the guys are going to see my face under those harsh fluorescent lights, my stomach just kind of ties itself into a knot. My heart will start beating a little harder and my palms get a little sweaty. \n\nBut to me, that just feels like a normal reaction to knowing you look terrible and that people are going to be staring at you, you know? Like, if your face was this lopsided, you'd probably be stressed out about going out in public, too. So I don't know if that’s an actual anxiety condition or if it's just... reality. Just me reacting to the facts of the situation. So, yeah, I honestly don't know if I'm clinically depressed or having significant anxiety or whatever the medical terms for it would be. I just know I'm completely miserable because of how crooked my jaw is, and it feels like it's never going to get better. I just feel totally stuck. You are asking if maybe I am seeing one thing and the rest of the world is seeing something completely different. And sure, logically, maybe? Because like I was just saying a minute ago, they do tell me they do not see it. It’s not like I wake up and think, \"Oh, maybe it's fine today.\" It is constantly—and I mean constantly—on my mind. \n\nI'll be walking down the main aisle at the warehouse, carrying a heavy box of inventory or pushing a cart, and I'm just bracing myself because I feel like everyone I pass is noticing it. Or, you know, like I mentioned with salsa night... I'd be in the middle of a crowded floor, the music is blasting, people are moving everywhere, and instead of just enjoying the dance, I'm completely consumed by this feeling that my partner is staring right at my lopsided jaw. And like I said before, even when they explicitly tell me they don't see anything, even when they promise me I look fine... it doesn't matter. The thoughts don't stop. I just keep thinking they're noticing it and just lying to my face to spare my feelings. \n\nSo... is it possible my face is perfectly normal and this isn't real? I don't even know how to answer that anymore. All I know is I can't stop obsessing over it. It's like I'm trapped in this loop where my face is messed up, everyone sees it, and nothing anyone says can convince me otherwise. It just... it ruins my whole day, every single day. From the minute I wake up to the minute I clock out at the warehouse, it's all I can focus on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your face appears normal to others, but you're seeing it differently?\n\nPatient: Doc, I... I mean, respectfully, we are literally going in circles here, man. You keep asking me the same question just wrapped up in different words, about whether I'm seeing things differently than everyone else. And I keep trying to explain to you that I’m here, sitting in this chair, specifically because I can’t stop thinking that the left side of my face looks totally crooked and messed up. That is the entire reason I came to see a doctor in the first place. \n\nIt’s like a broken record playing in my head that I can't unplug. Whether I'm at the warehouse scanning barcodes and wrapping pallets, or if I'm trying to go to salsa night just to have one normal evening... I just have this overwhelming, sickening feeling that everyone is noticing it. Every single person. And yeah, I know I already told you that they tell me they don’t see anything. The guys at work will be like, \"Man, you look fine, just pass the tape gun,\" or my dance partners will swear they didn't even notice my jaw. But it literally does not matter what they say to me. I hear the words coming out of their mouths, but my brain just completely rejects it. \n\nBecause when I look in the mirror before I leave the apartment, it's right there. It's so obvious, so glaringly messed up. So when you ask if it's possible my face is normal and I'm just seeing it differently... I mean, maybe? But how am I supposed to just accept that when the feeling is this intense? It's not just a passing thought, it's like this heavy, suffocating blanket over my entire life. I can't even focus on stacking boxes right because I'm too busy angling my head a certain way so my coworkers don't catch the bad side. I can't enjoy the rhythm of the music at the club because I'm sweating bullets, completely convinced everyone is staring at my lopsided cheek. So, I honestly don't know what else to tell you. I'm just here because I can't stop obsessing over it, and it feels like it's ruining my life, no matter how many times people swear to me that I look normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your face is not based on how it actually looks?\n\nPatient: Doc, are you... are you messing with me right now? Seriously, man, I feel like I'm taking crazy pills here. You're asking me the exact same thing for the fourth time. Is this a test? Because I honestly don't know how many different ways I can answer this for you. \n\nLook, like I have been saying this whole entire time... I am here, in this office, because I just cannot stop thinking that the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. That is the bottom line. Whether my perception is technically off or not, it doesn't change the fact that this is destroying my day-to-day life. I can't escape it. I'll be at the warehouse, literally just trying to load a truck or wrap a pallet so I can pay my rent, or I'll be trying to force myself to go to salsa night just to feel like a normal guy for two seconds... and the whole time, my stomach is in absolute knots because I feel like everyone is noticing it. Everyone. Every single person who glances in my direction. \n\nAnd yes, like we've established over and over, even when they look me dead in the eye and tell me they don't see anything wrong with my face, it doesn't fix it. It doesn't make the thoughts go away or make me believe them. It's just this constant, heavy obsession that I can't shake, no matter how you phrase the question or how many times we go over it. I just can't stop thinking about it, man. It's always there.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to enjoy things you used to like because of your concerns about your face?\n\nPatient: Oh, man... \"harder to enjoy\" doesn't even begin to cover it. It's... it's honestly bleeding into every single corner of my life right now. \n\nLike I was telling you about salsa night earlier, right? Well, the truth is, I haven't actually been in weeks. I used to go every Thursday, religiously. I loved the music, the energy, just... getting out of my own head for a few hours. I just can't deal with people looking at me or taking pictures anymore. It's way too much pressure.\n\nAnd it’s not even just the fun stuff, doc. It’s my job. It's my actual livelihood. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Irrational? I mean... I don't know. I honestly don't know how to answer that, doc. Unreasonable, irrational... those are just words, you know? To me, it's just my reality. 

I don't know if it's unreasonable. I mean, I guess part of me knows that other people don't seem to react to it the way I do. My sister says I'm crazy, my mom says I'm being ridiculous, and you're obviously asking me all these questions hinting that it's all in my head. But... when I wake up at 4:30 in the morning for my shift, and I go into my bathroom to brush my teeth, and I turn on that harsh overhead fluorescent light... I'm not *trying* to be obsessed. I don't want to be. I'm just looking at my reflection, and the left side is just... it's completely off. It's crooked. 

And I'll just stand there. I'll literally stand there with the toothbrush in my hand, the water just running and running down the drain, for like twenty, maybe thirty minutes. Just staring at it. Pushing on my skin, tilting my head, trying to see what everyone else claims they see. Trying to see a normal guy. But I can't. I just see this messed up jawline. 

So is it unreasonable that I panic about it? I really don't know. If you woke up and your face looked warped to you in the mirror every single morning, wouldn't you freak out? Wouldn't you try to hide it from the guys at the warehouse? I don't sit around *wanting* to think about this all day. I would love nothing more than to just throw some cold water on my face, grab my keys, and head out the door like I used to a couple years ago. But it's like my brain gets completely hijacked the second I see my reflection, and then I can't unsee it for the rest of the day. 

So... I don't know. I really don't know if it's irrational. I don't have a good answer for that. I just know it's there, and I can't stop seeing it, whether it makes sense to you or anyone else or not.

The way the left cheekbone seems lower, or maybe it's the jawline on that side that's just pulled down, making the whole thing look totally lopsided. I can spend, like, hours just staring at that one side, trying to figure out if it's getting worse, or if it's always been this bad and I just didn't notice it as much before. I'll take pictures on my phone from different angles, trying to catch it in different lighting, just to prove to myself that I'm not crazy, that it really is as crooked as I think it is. But yeah, if you're asking if I have issues with anything below the neck, or even the right side of my face, no. It's just this one terrible, glaring flaw on the left side that I can't escape. It's like my eyes are just drawn to it immediately, and I can't look at anything else when I see my reflection.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the left side of your face caused you to avoid any social or work-related activities?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah... yeah, it has. It really has, and it's honestly ruining everything. I mean, at work, it's gotten so bad that I've actually been written up. My supervisor pulled me into the office last week because my numbers were down. And I tried to explain that I was just... distracted, but I couldn't tell him the real reason. The truth is, I keep stopping to check my reflection in the breakroom mirror, or I'll go to the bathroom just to look at my face under the lights in there, trying to see if the shadows make it look worse. And then I get stuck there, you know? I'll be in there for twenty minutes, just staring, trying to push my skin up to see if it fixes the crookedness, and meanwhile, my orders are piling up. So yeah, my work is definitely suffering because I just can't focus on pulling stock when I know everyone walking past me is probably staring at my face.\n\nAnd outside of work, it's... it's basically stopped my life completely. Like I mentioned before, I used to go to those salsa nights on Thursdays, right? I loved it. I was getting really good, actually. But I haven't been in over a month. I just can't do it anymore. The thought of being that close to a partner, with them looking right at me... I can't handle it. I even stopped playing in my Sunday soccer league. I told the guys I pulled a hamstring, but really, I just couldn't stand being out on the field in the bright daylight where there's nowhere to hide my face. \n\nIt's not just hobbies, either. I've been skipping family gatherings, too. My sister had a barbecue a couple of weeks ago for her birthday, and I made up some excuse about feeling sick. I stayed home and basically just sat in the dark. I’ll adjust it a dozen times. And while I'm doing that, I just start rubbing at my face. It’s always this one exact spot on the left side, right along the jawline. I just keep rubbing and pressing on it with my thumb, almost like... I don't know, like I’m hoping if I press hard enough, I can push the bone back into place or smooth out the lopsidedness. I’ll rub it until the skin gets all raw and red, which I know probably draws more attention to it, but in the moment, I just can't stop myself from doing it. \n\nAnd the worst part is, I lose all track of time. I'll think I've been in there for two minutes, but it's been twenty. So I end up being super late getting back to my aisle. He’ll come track me down in the aisles with his clipboard, asking why my pick rate is in the toilet for the last hour and where I wandered off to. So, yeah, I'm avoiding pretty much everything now because I just can't stand the thought of people looking at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions or rituals in an attempt to alleviate your concerns about the left side of your face?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I definitely do. It's like I can't control it, honestly. I know I mentioned the breakroom earlier, but it’s really bad in the actual restrooms at the warehouse. Like, right before I have to go back out on the inventory floor—after my fifteen-minute break or my lunch, or even if I just sneak away to use the urinal—I get completely trapped at the sinks. I just keep checking my face in that bathroom mirror. The lighting in there is this sickly, harsh fluorescent, and I just stand there, leaning over the sink, staring right at the left side of my face. \n\nAnd I wear this company-issued baseball cap, right? We all have to wear them. So I’ll stand there fixing my cap, pulling the brim down low over my eyes, then pushing it back up, tilting it to the left, trying to see if I can angle the shadow to hide how crooked my cheek is. I’ll adjust it a dozen times. And while I'm doing that, I just start rubbing at my face. It’s always this one exact spot on the left side, right along the jawline. I just keep rubbing and pressing on it with my thumb, almost like... I don't know, like I’m hoping if I press hard enough, I can push the bone back into place or smooth out the lopsidedness. I’ll rub it until the skin gets all raw and red, which I know probably draws more attention to it, but in the moment, I just can't stop myself from doing it. \n\nAnd the worst part is, I lose all track of time. I'll think I've been in there for two minutes, but it's been twenty. So I end up being super late getting back to my aisle. At the warehouse, everything is tracked by these handheld scanners, so they know exactly how many items you pull per hour. Because I'm stuck in the bathroom doing this whole routine with the mirror and the hat and rubbing my face, my picking numbers just completely fall behind. And then my boss gets on me again. He’ll come track me down in the aisles with his clipboard, asking why my pick rate is in the toilet for the last hour and where I wandered off to. I can't exactly tell him the truth, so I just have to stand there and take it, knowing I'm probably going to do the exact same thing the next time I walk past a mirror.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other areas of your life, beyond work and social activities, where you feel your concerns about your face are affecting your ability to function or causing you distress?\n\nPatient: I mean, when you say other areas... I'm trying to think, but honestly, what else is there? Like, work and social stuff, that basically makes up a person's entire life, you know? At least, it makes up mine. I’m a single guy, thirty years old, I don't have kids or a mortgage or anything like that. So my routine has always just been waking up, going to the warehouse, putting in my hours, and then having a little fun on the weekends with the soccer guys or Thursday nights with the salsa dancing. But now that both of those things are completely falling apart... I don't really have anything else. It's just... a void, I guess. \n\nI mean, I suppose you could say it affects my downtime at home, if that counts as an area of life? Because even when I'm completely alone in my apartment, with the blinds pulled down and no one around to look at me or judge me, I'm still not relaxed. I'm still just sitting on the edge of my bed or on the couch, pulling up the front-facing camera on my phone, staring at the screen, zooming in on my left cheekbone. I'll try to watch a movie or play a video game or something just to distract myself, but my mind just keeps drifting right back to my jawline. I'll literally pause the TV just to go into the bathroom and check the mirror again, even though I know I'm the only one in the apartment. So, I guess it ruins my peace of mind, my ability to just sit and exist without panicking about how lopsided my face is. \n\nBut as far as like, other actual responsibilities or functioning? No, not really. I still manage to pay my rent online, I still do my laundry, even if I'm just rushing down to the apartment complex basement late at night when I think no one else is down there so I don't have to make eye contact. I'm not failing at basic survival or anything. It's just that the things that actually made life feel like a life—hanging out with my sister, playing sports, doing a good job at work and maybe moving up to get my forklift certification someday—all of that is just stalled out. It feels like it's gone. Because I can't get past this one glaring thing on my face. So, yeah, I think work and being around people pretty much covers the extent of the damage, because without those, I'm really just a guy sitting alone in a room obsessing over his reflection.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe your face isn't actually as crooked as you think it is?\n\nPatient: I mean... I try to tell myself that sometimes. I really do try to rationalize it. Because, honestly, that's what other people say. If I ever kind of hint at it, or if I've flat out asked one of the guys at the warehouse, or back when I used to go to salsa night and I'd ask a partner if I looked alright... they always say the exact same thing. They tell me they don't see anything. They'll say, \"Man, you look fine, what are you even talking about?\" \n\nAnd for like, a split second, I want to believe them. I want to think, okay, maybe I'm blowing this out of proportion, maybe it's not that bad. But then... the feeling just comes rushing right back. Because I know what I see, you know? I can't just ignore my own eyes. I look in the mirror and the left side of my face is just so clearly crooked and messed up. It's glaring to me. So even when they look me dead in the eye and swear they don't see anything wrong, my brain just tells me they're lying. I figure they're just being polite, or they pity me, or they just don't want to make things awkward by agreeing that I look terrible. \n\nBecause I just can't stop thinking about it. It's like this tape playing on a loop in my head every single second of the day. I’m just constantly convinced that everyone at the warehouse is noticing it when I walk down the aisle, or that everyone at salsa night was staring right at my lopsided cheek the whole time we were dancing. So, no matter what anyone tells me, I just... I can't shake it. That's why I'm here, honestly, because I just can't stop obsessing over how crooked and messed up my face is, and I can't turn off the feeling that everyone is staring at it, even when they swear to me they don't see a thing.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing significant anxiety or depression as a result of your concerns about your face?\n\nPatient: Anxiety or depression... I mean, I don't really know how to answer that. I'm not entirely sure what counts as, like, official depression or a real anxiety problem, you know? I've never been the kind of guy to go to a therapist or read up on mental health stuff before all this. I just... I know I feel like absolute garbage most of the time. But is it depression? I really couldn't tell you. I still get out of bed, I still put on my steel-toe boots and my safety vest and drive my car to the warehouse every single morning. I'm not just laying under the covers crying all day or anything like that. \n\nBut at the same time, it’s just this heavy, exhausting feeling that never really goes away. Like, I was saying earlier about sitting alone in my apartment... I'll just sit on this old brown hand-me-down couch my sister gave me when I moved in, and I just feel completely drained. Just wiped out. Like there's this physical weight sitting right on my chest. And when I think about having to go back to work the next day and punch in at the time clock, knowing the guys are going to see my face under those harsh fluorescent lights, my stomach just kind of ties itself into a knot. My heart will start beating a little harder and my palms get a little sweaty. \n\nBut to me, that just feels like a normal reaction to knowing you look terrible and that people are going to be staring at you, you know? Like, if your face was this lopsided, you'd probably be stressed out about going out in public, too. So I don't know if that’s an actual anxiety condition or if it's just... reality. Just me reacting to the facts of the situation. So, yeah, I honestly don't know if I'm clinically depressed or having significant anxiety or whatever the medical terms for it would be. I just know I'm completely miserable because of how crooked my jaw is, and it feels like it's never going to get better. I just feel totally stuck. But I don't really have a clinical name for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your face being crooked might not match how others see you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know we just kind of touched on this, and I get what you are asking. You are asking if maybe I am seeing one thing and the rest of the world is seeing something completely different. And sure, logically, maybe? Because like I was just saying a minute ago, they do tell me they do not see it. The guys at the warehouse, the people at salsa night... they say my face looks fine. \n\nBut... and this is exactly why I am here, sitting in this chair talking to you right now... I just cannot stop thinking about it. I cannot turn off this absolute certainty that the left side of my face is completely crooked and messed up. It is not like I want to feel this way. I don't want to walk into the breakroom at work to grab a water and feel this intense, burning sensation that every single person eating their sandwich is staring right at my jawline. I don't want to be out on the dance floor, or well, back when I used to actually go to salsa night, trying to remember the steps, but all I can focus on is whether my partner is secretly grossed out by how lopsided my cheek is. \n\nEven when they swear up and down they don't see anything, my brain just screams at me that they are just being polite or looking the other way out of pity. So, is it possible my perception doesn't match theirs? I guess, technically, sure. But it doesn't change anything for me in my day-to-day life. It doesn't stop the thoughts from running a mile a minute. I am just trapped in my own head, constantly obsessing over how messed up it looks, and no matter how many times someone says I look normal, it just bounces right off me. I just can't stop thinking that everyone is noticing it. It is just always there, you know? Like this giant spotlight on the side of my face that only I can feel, but I am convinced everyone else is staring right at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the left side of your face is actually perfectly normal and your perception of it being crooked is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I feel like... I mean, I know we're kind of going over the exact same ground here, doc. Like I was just saying, I get the logic of what you're asking. You're asking if I'm just straight-up making this up in my head or something, if it's not based in reality at all. But... to say it's \"perfectly normal\"? I just, I can't wrap my head around that. I really can't. \n\nBecause, look, the whole reason I made this appointment, the whole reason I'm even sitting in this office right now, is because I just can't stop thinking that the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. I can't just turn the volume down on it. It’s not like I wake up and think, \"Oh, maybe it's fine today.\" It is constantly—and I mean constantly—on my mind. \n\nI'll be walking down the main aisle at the warehouse, carrying a heavy box of inventory or pushing a cart, and I'm just bracing myself because I feel like everyone I pass is noticing it. I don't even know how to answer that anymore. All I know is I can't stop obsessing over it. From the minute I wake up to the minute I clock out at the warehouse, it's all I can focus on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your face appears normal to others, but you're seeing it differently?\n\nPatient: Doc, I... I mean, respectfully, we are literally going in circles here, man. You keep asking me the same question just wrapped up in different words, about whether I'm seeing things differently than everyone else. And I keep trying to explain to you that I’m here, sitting in this chair, specifically because I can’t stop thinking that the left side of my face looks totally crooked and messed up. Whether I'm at the warehouse scanning barcodes and wrapping pallets, or if I'm trying to go to salsa night just to have one normal evening... I just have this overwhelming, sickening feeling that everyone is noticing it. Every single person. And yeah, I know I already told you that they tell me they don’t see anything. The guys at work will be like, \"Man, you look fine, just pass the tape gun,\" or my dance partners will swear they didn't even notice my jaw. But it literally does not matter what they say to me. It's so obvious, so glaringly messed up. So when you ask if it's possible my face is normal and I'm just seeing it differently... I mean, maybe? I can't even focus on stacking boxes right because I'm too busy angling my head a certain way so my coworkers don't catch the bad side. I can't enjoy the rhythm of the music at the club because I'm sweating bullets, completely convinced everyone is staring at my lopsided cheek. So, I honestly don't know what else to tell you.
