I just can't stop thinking about it, man. It's always there.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to enjoy things you used to like because of your concerns about your face?\n\nPatient: Oh, man... \"harder to enjoy\" doesn't even begin to cover it. It's... it's honestly bleeding into every single corner of my life right now. \n\nLike I was telling you about salsa night earlier, right? Well, the truth is, I haven't actually been in weeks. I used to go every Thursday, religiously. I loved the music, the energy, just... getting out of my own head for a few hours. But now, just the thought of being under those club lights, knowing everyone is going to be staring at my face... I just can't do it. I've started skipping it entirely. \n\nAnd it's not just dancing. I play—well, I *used* to play—in this Sunday league soccer thing with some guys from the neighborhood. But I stopped showing up to that, too. And family gatherings? Forget about it. My sister had a get-together recently and I just made up some excuse about feeling sick, because I know my aunt is always pulling out her phone to take pictures. The idea of my crooked jaw being immortalized in a photo, where everyone can just zoom in and stare at how messed up it is... it literally makes my stomach turn. I just can't deal with people looking at me or taking pictures anymore. It's way too much pressure.\n\nAnd it’s not even just the fun stuff, doc. It’s my job. It's my actual livelihood. Because I'm so distracted, so intensely focused on making sure nobody is catching my bad side, I'm moving way slower. I'll literally stand there at the end of an aisle, waiting for people to pass before I go grab an item, just so they don't see me. And because of that, my work gets delayed. And then my supervisor is on my case, pulling me into the office, and I have a literal disciplinary paper in my file now after years of being a good worker. Over this. Because I can't focus.\n\nAnd my personal life? It's the exact same thing. I’ve started skipping everything—the Sunday soccer games with the guys, the Thursday salsa nights, even my own family gatherings. I just... I can't handle it. I can't put myself in a situation where I have to deal with people looking at me, or my aunt pulling out her phone and taking pictures of everyone. The thought of a camera flashing and catching my face, freezing how messed up it is so everyone can just stare at it later... it makes me want to crawl out of my skin. I can't do it. \n\nSo yeah, asking if it's \"interfering with my ability to function\" is kind of an understatement. It's basically stopped my entire life in its tracks. I don't function like a normal person anymore, because every single decision I make is based on how I can avoid people seeing my face.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your obsession with the left side of your face is unreasonable or irrational?\n\nPatient: Irrational? I mean... I don't know. I honestly don't know how to answer that, doc. Unreasonable, irrational... those are just words, you know? To me, it's just my reality. \n\nI don't know if it's unreasonable. My sister says I'm crazy, my mom says I'm being ridiculous, and you're obviously asking me all these questions hinting that it's all in my head. But... when I wake up at 4:30 in the morning for my shift, and I go into my bathroom to brush my teeth, and I turn on that harsh overhead fluorescent light... I'm not *trying* to be obsessed. I don't want to be. I'm just looking at my reflection, and the left side is just... it's completely off. It's crooked. \n\nAnd I'll just stand there. I'll literally stand there with the toothbrush in my hand, the water just running and running down the drain, for like twenty, maybe thirty minutes. Just staring at it. Pushing on my skin, tilting my head, trying to see what everyone else claims they see. Trying to see a normal guy. But I can't. I just see this messed up jawline. \n\nSo is it unreasonable that I panic about it? I really don't know. I don't sit around *wanting* to think about this all day. I would love nothing more than to just throw some cold water on my face, grab my keys, and head out the door like I used to a couple years ago. But it's like my brain gets completely hijacked the second I see my reflection, and then I can't unsee it for the rest of the day. \n\nSo... I don't know. I really don't know if it's irrational. I don't have a good answer for that. I just know it's there, and I can't stop seeing it, whether it makes sense to you or anyone else or not.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Distress... man, yeah. It’s just... it’s this constant, heavy weight, you know? Like, I don't even know how to fully explain the toll it takes on a person's mind to live like this. It's exhausting. It is so completely, utterly exhausting. 

I’ll be at work, right? And like I said, I'm already hiding behind pallets and dodging people, which is stressful enough. But then lunch rolls around. And it used to be that lunch was the best part of the shift. We'd all go into the breakroom, grab a soda from the vending machine, maybe complain about the supervisor or talk about the game. Just normal guy stuff. But now? I can't even step foot in the breakroom because the fluorescent lighting in there is so bright and everyone is sitting right across from each other at those narrow little tables. There's nowhere to hide your face. 

So instead, I walk all the way out to the back of the parking lot, to my car. I have this old Honda, and I just sit in there. By myself. In the heat. And I just feel this... this horrible, sinking feeling in my gut. I'll pull down the sun visor and look in the little mirror, and I just get so overwhelmed with how messed up my jaw is, and how it’s ruining my life, that I just... I actually break down. I'm a thirty-year-old man, sitting in a baking hot car in a warehouse parking lot, just crying. Because I hate it so much. I hate how I look, I hate how it makes me feel, I hate that I'm losing my friends and getting written up at a job I used to be good at. 

It's like this dark cloud that just follows me everywhere, from the second I wake up to the second I finally manage to fall asleep. My stomach is always in knots. I just feel so miserable and tense all the time, and I'm so angry at myself for being this way, but I can't stop it. So, yeah... distress doesn't even feel like a big enough word for it. It's just tearing me apart inside.

Whether my perception is technically off or not, it doesn't change the fact that this is destroying my day-to-day life. I can't escape it. I'll be at the warehouse, literally just trying to load a truck or wrap a pallet so I can pay my rent, or I'll be trying to force myself to go to salsa night just to feel like a normal guy for two seconds... and the whole time, my stomach is in absolute knots because I feel like everyone is noticing it. Everyone. Every single person who glances in my direction. \n\nAnd yes, like we've established over and over, even when they look me dead in the eye and tell me they don't see anything wrong with my face, it doesn't fix it. It doesn't make the thoughts go away or make me believe them. It's just this constant, heavy obsession that I can't shake, no matter how you phrase the question or how many times we go over it. I just can't stop thinking about it, man. It's always there.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to enjoy things you used to like because of your concerns about your face?\n\nPatient: Oh, man... \"harder to enjoy\" doesn't even begin to cover it. It's... it's honestly bleeding into every single corner of my life right now. \n\nLike I was telling you about salsa night earlier, right? Well, the truth is, I haven't actually been in weeks. I used to go every Thursday, religiously. I loved the music, the energy, just... getting out of my own head for a few hours. But now, just the thought of being under those club lights, knowing everyone is going to be staring at my face... I just can't do it. I've started skipping it entirely. \n\nAnd it's not just dancing. I play—well, I *used* to play—in this Sunday league soccer thing with some guys from the neighborhood. But I stopped showing up to that, too. And family gatherings? Forget about it. My sister had a get-together recently and I just made up some excuse about feeling sick, because I know my aunt is always pulling out her phone to take pictures. The idea of my crooked jaw being immortalized in a photo, where everyone can just zoom in and stare at how messed up it is... it literally makes my stomach turn. I just can't deal with people looking at me or taking pictures anymore. It's way too much pressure.\n\nAnd it’s not even just the fun stuff, doc. It’s my job. It's my actual livelihood. I told you how I'm always trying to hide my face at work, angling my head when I'm pushing the cart or wrapping a pallet? Well, it's actually getting me in real trouble now. Because I'm so distracted, so intensely focused on making sure nobody is catching my bad side, I'm moving way slower. I'll literally stand there at the end of an aisle, waiting for people to pass before I go grab an item, just so they don't see me. And because of that, my work gets delayed. My supervisor actually pulled me into the office the other day and wrote me up. He formally wrote me up because my numbers are down. I've been at that warehouse for years and I've never had a write-up! But I just can't focus on filling orders when my brain is screaming at me all day. \n\nSo... yeah. I'm not enjoying anything. I'm just hiding.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like your concerns about your face are significantly interfering with your ability to function in daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, doc... I mean, yes. That is exactly what I literally just laid out for you. I don't know if I'm just not explaining it right, or if you have a checklist you have to get through, but yes, it is significantly interfering. \n\nLike I just said, I’ve been officially written up at the warehouse because my work gets delayed. I'm standing there, hiding behind pallets of dog food or whatever, waiting for the aisle to clear out so nobody sees my face, and my pick rate just completely tanks. And then my supervisor is on my case, pulling me into the office, and I have a literal disciplinary paper in my file now after years of being a good worker. Over this. Because I can't focus.\n\nAnd my personal life? It's the exact same thing. I’ve started skipping everything—the Sunday soccer games with the guys, the Thursday salsa nights, even my own family gatherings. I just... I can't handle it. I can't put myself in a situation where I have to deal with people looking at me, or my aunt pulling out her phone and taking pictures of everyone. The thought of a camera flashing and catching my face, freezing how messed up it is so everyone can just stare at it later... it makes me want to crawl out of my skin. I can't do it. \n\nSo yeah, asking if it's \"interfering with my ability to function\" is kind of an understatement. It's basically stopped my entire life in its tracks. I don't function like a normal person anymore, because every single decision I make is based on how I can avoid people seeing my face.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your obsession with the left side of your face is unreasonable or irrational?\n\nPatient: Irrational? I mean... I don't know. I honestly don't know how to answer that, doc. Unreasonable, irrational... those are just words, you know? To me, it's just my reality. \n\nI don't know if it's unreasonable. I mean, I guess part of me knows that other people don't seem to react to it the way I do. My sister says I'm crazy, my mom says I'm being ridiculous, and you're obviously asking me all these questions hinting that it's all in my head. But... when I wake up at 4:30 in the morning for my shift, and I go into my bathroom to brush my teeth, and I turn on that harsh overhead fluorescent light... I'm not *trying* to be obsessed. I don't want to be. I'm just looking at my reflection, and the left side is just... it's completely off. It's crooked. \n\nAnd I'll just stand there. I'll literally stand there with the toothbrush in my hand, the water just running and running down the drain, for like twenty, maybe thirty minutes. Just staring at it. Pushing on my skin, tilting my head, trying to see what everyone else claims they see. Trying to see a normal guy. But I can't. I just see this messed up jawline. \n\nSo is it unreasonable that I panic about it? I really don't know. If you woke up and your face looked warped to you in the mirror every single morning, wouldn't you freak out? Wouldn't you try to hide it from the guys at the warehouse? I don't sit around *wanting* to think about this all day. I would love nothing more than to just throw some cold water on my face, grab my keys, and head out the door like I used to a couple years ago. But it's like my brain gets completely hijacked the second I see my reflection, and then I can't unsee it for the rest of the day. \n\nSo... I don't know. I really don't know if it's irrational. I don't have a good answer for that. I just know it's there, and I can't stop seeing it, whether it makes sense to you or anyone else or not.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing significant emotional distress as a result of your concerns about your face?\n\nPatient: Distress... man, yeah. It’s just... it’s this constant, heavy weight, you know? Like, I don't even know how to fully explain the toll it takes on a person's mind to live like this. It's exhausting. It is so completely, utterly exhausting. \n\nI’ll be at work, right? And like I said, I'm already hiding behind pallets and dodging people, which is stressful enough. But then lunch rolls around. And it used to be that lunch was the best part of the shift. We'd all go into the breakroom, grab a soda from the vending machine, maybe complain about the supervisor or talk about the game. Just normal guy stuff. But now? I can't even step foot in the breakroom because the fluorescent lighting in there is so bright and everyone is sitting right across from each other at those narrow little tables. There's nowhere to hide your face. \n\nSo instead, I walk all the way out to the back of the parking lot, to my car. I have this old Honda, and I just sit in there. By myself. In the heat. And I just feel this... this horrible, sinking feeling in my gut. I'll pull down the sun visor and look in the little mirror, and I just get so overwhelmed with how messed up my jaw is, and how it’s ruining my life, that I just... I actually break down. I'm a thirty-year-old man, sitting in a baking hot car in a warehouse parking lot, just crying. Because I hate it so much. I hate how I look, I hate how it makes me feel, I hate that I'm losing my friends and getting written up at a job I used to be good at. \n\nIt's like this dark cloud that just follows me everywhere, from the second I wake up to the second I finally manage to fall asleep. My stomach is always in knots. I just feel so miserable and tense all the time, and I'm so angry at myself for being this way, but I can't stop it. So, yeah... distress doesn't even feel like a big enough word for it. It's just tearing me apart inside.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Oh, God, yes. Absolutely. I mean, pictures are actually the absolute worst for me. If you took a picture of me right now and slid it across this desk, I wouldn't just see it, it's literally the only thing I would be able to focus on. Honestly, that's the whole reason I'm even here in this office with you today. I just... I can’t stop thinking that the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. It's constantly on my mind, just playing on a loop. 

And it's like, I just know everyone at the warehouse is noticing it. Or, well, back when I used to actually go to Thursday salsa night, I could just feel everybody looking at it when I'd try to dance. I'd be out there on the floor, trying to remember the steps or just trying to have a good time, but my brain would just be screaming that everyone is staring at my jaw and judging how asymmetrical I am. 

And the most frustrating part of the whole thing is that when I actually bring it up to people? Like if I show a picture on my phone to my buddy Dave at the warehouse, and I zoom in and point right to the crooked spot? They tell me they don’t see anything. They swear up and down they don't notice it. My mom does the same thing. But I know they're just being polite, or maybe they just don't want to make me feel bad, or they're just trying to get me to shut up about it. Because how could you not see it? It's right there. 

So, yeah, to answer your question, a picture wouldn't fix anything. It just freezes it so I can stare at how messed up it is even longer without having to hold a mirror up. I'd definitely still see it. I can't *not* see it, no matter what anyone else claims.

Specifically, it's the left side of my face. It's just completely crooked and... and messed up. Like, if you look at it—well, maybe the lighting in here isn't showing it exactly right, but I know it's there. It's like the whole left side just doesn't line up with the right side at all. \n\nAnd it's ruining my life, really. I work as an order filler at this massive warehouse, right? Honestly, that's the whole reason I'm even here in this office with you today. I just... I can’t stop thinking that the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. It's constantly on my mind, just playing on a loop. \n\nAnd it's like, I just know everyone at the warehouse is noticing it. Or, well, back when I used to actually go to Thursday salsa night, I could just feel everybody looking at it when I'd try to dance. I'd be out there on the floor, trying to remember the steps or just trying to have a good time, but my brain would just be screaming that everyone is staring at my jaw and judging how asymmetrical I am. \n\nAnd the most frustrating part of the whole thing is that when I actually bring it up to people? Like if I show a picture on my phone to my buddy Dave at the warehouse, and I zoom in and point right to the crooked spot? They tell me they don’t see anything. They swear up and down they don't notice it. My mom does the same thing. But I know they're just being polite, or maybe they just don't want to make me feel bad, or they're just trying to get me to shut up about it. Because how could you not see it? It's right there. \n\nSo, yeah, to answer your question, a picture wouldn't fix anything. It just freezes it so I can stare at how messed up it is even longer without having to hold a mirror up. I'd definitely still see it. I can't *not* see it, no matter what anyone else claims."
  }
